Absolutely — this is the best question you could ask. Let me walk you through the whole thing like a story, so the "flow" makes sense and you can repeat it on any claim.

---

# 🎯 The Big Picture First

Your job on any A/R follow-up claim is to answer 3 questions:

1. **What was billed and what happened to it?** (Paid? Denied? Pending?)
2. **Does anyone still owe money?** (Insurance? Patient? Nobody?)
3. **Does any action need to be taken?** (Appeal? Rebill? Call insurance? Nothing?)

Everything you look at — EMR, ERA, portal, AMD — is just to answer those 3 questions. Then you write a comment and pick a category.

---

# 🗺️ The Flow We Followed (Step by Step)

## STEP 1 — Start in Excel, get the claim info

**What we did:** Looked at the Excel row for Roxanne Ramos — claim ID R18976, UHC, DOS 11/12/2025, billed $650, paid $317.

**Why:** This is your starting point. Excel tells you *which* claim to investigate. Copy the Patient ID or Claim ID from here.

**What to look at:**
- Claim ID (this opens the claim in EMR)
- Patient ID
- Billed amount vs Paid amount → if they don't match, something happened (denial, adjustment, partial pay, etc.)

---

## STEP 2 — Open EMR → Manage Patient Transactions

**What we did:** Pasted the patient ID in EMR, landed on the "Manage Patient Transactions" screen.

**Why:** EMR is your **home base**. It shows the office's version of the truth — what was billed, what was received, what's still open.

**What to look at (in order):**

| Section | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| **Charges row** (top) | Billed amount, CPT, ICD, DOS, provider |
| **Total Bal. / PRI / SEC / PAT** | Who owes what right now (Primary ins / Secondary ins / Patient) |
| **Transaction rows** (bottom) | Every payment, adjustment, refund posted so far |
| **A/R Note** (right side box) | Latest note by a biller — always says "More" if longer |
| **Eligibility Status** | Whether insurance is active for this DOS |

👉 **First thing you always check: the balance.** If it's $0, the claim looks closed on paper — but a note or ERA might still show something unresolved (like our case).

---

## STEP 3 — Read the Biller Notes & A/R Followup Log

**What we did:** Clicked the "More" link and the AR Followup Log to read all previous notes.

**Why:** Notes are the **memory of the account**. Other billers before you have worked this claim — reading their notes tells you:
- What's already been done
- What's pending
- What the insurance said last time

**Never skip this step.** Doing work someone else already did = wasted time. Missing a note = you might redo something wrong.

**What to look at:**
- Dates of every note (oldest → newest)
- Reference numbers (call ref#, DCN#, case ID#)
- Names of insurance reps spoken to
- What action was taken (faxed MR, appealed, called, etc.)
- What insurance told the biller

**Vocab we learned here:**
- **MR** = Medical Records
- **DCN** = Document Control Number (ID for records ins received)
- **TAT** = Turn Around Time
- **ref#** = Call reference number (proof the call happened)
- **s/w** = Spoke With
- **Optum** = UHC's review/audit arm

---

## STEP 4 — Open the Associated ERA

**What we did:** Clicked "Associated ERA" and opened both ERA files (there were two).

**Why:** The ERA is the **insurance's official explanation** of what they did with the claim. It's more reliable than any note — it's straight from the insurance system.

If EMR shows a payment or adjustment, the ERA tells you **WHY**.

**What to look at on any ERA:**

| Column | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| **Status line** (e.g., "Processed as primary", "Reversal of previous payment") | The single most important field — tells you what UHC did |
| **Billed** | What was submitted |
| **Allowed** | What UHC agrees the service is worth |
| **Paid** | What UHC actually paid |
| **Deduct / CoIns / CoPay** | Patient's share of the responsibility |
| **Adj Code** (like PR-242, CO-45, PI-251) | Why UHC didn't pay the full amount |
| **Rem Code** (like N705, MA15) | Extra detail about the decision |

**Adj Code prefixes you'll see a lot:**
- **PR** = Patient Responsibility (patient owes it)
- **CO** = Contractual Obligation (write-off, patient doesn't owe)
- **PI** = Payer Initiated (insurance's own decision, usually write-off)
- **OA** = Other Adjustment

👉 **If there are multiple ERAs for the same claim, read them in date order** — they tell you the sequence of what happened.

---

## STEP 5 — Check the Insurance Portal (UHC in this case)

**What we did:** Logged into UHC's provider portal, searched by Member ID, opened the claim.

**Why:** This is the **live, current, official source of truth from the insurance**. EMR and ERAs might be old — the portal shows what UHC's system says *right now*.

Always check the portal when:
- The EMR/ERA story is unclear
- Notes are old (like ours — Zeenal's last call was 05/07, today is 07/24)
- You need to know the current status
- You need to check for appeal deadlines

**What to look at on any insurance portal:**

| Section | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| **Search results list** | All claims for this patient (spot duplicates!) |
| **Current claim status** | Finalized / Denied / Pending / Rejected / Action Required |
| **Adjudication status** | In-network vs Out-of-network (affects patient responsibility) |
| **Claim history timeline** | Every date something changed |
| **Claim codes / Remark codes** | Why UHC did what they did |
| **Line items** (processing history) | Every reprocess attempt with dates and amounts |
| **Billing summary** | Final totals: billed, adjustments, patient resp, paid |
| **Payments section** | Check numbers, payment dates |
| **Documents section** | Letters UHC sent (denial letters, request letters) |
| **Coordination of benefits** | Any other insurance we should know about |

👉 **Cross-check EMR payment vs Portal payment.** If they match → good. If they don't → something is missing or misposted.

---

## STEP 6 — Check AMD for Demographics & Secondary Insurance

**What we did:** Looked at AMD (Advanced MD) to confirm patient info and check for secondary insurance.

**Why:** EMR sometimes doesn't have the latest patient/insurance info. AMD is the **patient's main chart** — it has demographics, insurance policies, and history.

**What to look at:**
- **Primary insurance** — matches what we billed?
- **Secondary insurance** — is there one? (If yes, we may need to bill them too)
- **Policy #** and **Group #** — match what was billed?
- **Effective dates** — was insurance active on the DOS?
- **Patient's chart / Timeline** — any recent notes or documents

👉 If there's a **secondary insurance**, you may need to bill them next (called "**crossover**" or "**secondary billing**") after the primary pays.

---

## STEP 7 — Put It All Together & Decide

**What we did:** Combined every piece of info to build the full timeline, then decided the claim status.

**Why:** No single window tells you the whole story. EMR + Notes + ERA + Portal + AMD together = the truth.

**The mental checklist:**
1. What did we bill?
2. Did insurance pay, deny, or pend it?
3. If denied/pended — was follow-up done? Is more action needed?
4. Is the balance really $0, or does someone still owe money?
5. Is the account fully resolved, or does it need more work?

---

## STEP 8 — Write the Comment & Pick the Category in Excel

**What we did:** Wrote a detailed comment summarizing everything, picked the status "Paid / Closed."

**Why:** The Excel is your **deliverable**. Your comment should let anyone (your senior, a future biller, an auditor) understand the entire story of the claim **without re-doing your research**.

**A good comment always includes:**
- Claim number / ICN
- DOS, CPT, billed amount
- What insurance did (paid / denied / pending)
- Any reference numbers (DCN, case ID, call ref#)
- Actions taken by previous billers
- Final outcome (payment amount, patient responsibility, adjustment)
- **What's the next step** — or "No further action required, claim closed"

---

# 🧠 The Flow in ONE Simple Sentence

> **Excel tells you which claim → EMR shows what your office knows → Notes tell you what's already been done → ERA explains what insurance did and why → Portal confirms the current status straight from insurance → AMD checks demographics and secondary insurance → You combine it all → Write comment → Pick category → Done.**

---

# 🎁 One Habit That Will Save You Every Time

**Always go in this order: EMR → Notes → ERA → Portal → AMD.**

Not because you'll always need all five, but because each step *tells you what to check next*. If EMR shows $0 balance and notes say "closed, paid" — you might not need the portal. If EMR shows a denial — the ERA tells you why → portal tells you if you can appeal → AMD tells you if there's another insurance to bill.

**Follow the story. Let each window guide you to the next.** 🙂

🎯 Perfect — this is a really common case, and it's actually **easier** than the last one because there's no back-and-forth story. But we DO need to decode what UHC is telling us. Let me walk you through it.

## What I can see from the ERA

For patient **KIERSTINE MEYER, MRN 1029194, Claim R18936, DOS 11/03/2025**:

| CPT | Billed | Paid | Adj Code | Adj Amount |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 99213 (25) | $650.00 | $0.00 | **PR-26** | $650.00 |
| 20552 | $1,034.30 | $0.00 | **PR-26** | $1,034.30 |
| 20611 (LT) | $8,337.76 | $0.00 | **PR-26** | $8,337.76 |
| J1010 (40) | $50.00 | $0.00 | **PR-26** | $50.00 |
| **Total** | **$10,072.06** | **$0.00** | | **$10,072.06** |

**Status: Processed as primary. Total Paid: $0.00. Patient Responsibility: $10,072.06.**

## 🔑 Decoding the code — PR-26

**PR-26** means: **"Expenses incurred prior to coverage"** — meaning UHC is saying the patient's insurance was **NOT active on the date of service (11/03/2025)**.

- **PR** = Patient Responsibility (technically UHC is saying "not our problem, patient owes")
- **26** = specific reason: coverage wasn't in effect on that date

*(You'll see similar denials with different codes: **PR-27** = "Expenses after coverage terminated", **PR-31** = "Patient not identified", etc. But PR-26 specifically = policy wasn't active yet.)*

---

## 🧩 So the story is simple

UHC denied the ENTIRE claim ($10,072.06) saying the patient's insurance wasn't active on 11/03/2025. Now we need to figure out **what to do about it.**

## 👉 What to check next (the order matters here)

Since there are no notes/logs, YOU are the first biller working this. Here's what to check:

### Step 1: Check EMR Manage Patient Transactions
Go back to the main EMR screen for this patient. Tell me:
- What's the current **Total Balance**?
- What's the **PRI / SEC / PAT** breakdown?
- Is there anything already posted (payment, adjustment, refund)?
- Is there a **secondary insurance** listed at the top?

### Step 2: Check Eligibility on the UHC portal
This is the **key step** for a PR-26 denial. We need to verify:
- Was the patient's UHC policy really inactive on 11/03/2025?
- When did the policy become active?
- Is it active today?

### Step 3: Check AMD for other insurance
Sometimes when primary was inactive, there's another insurance the patient forgot to mention. AMD might have it.

---

Then we'll head to the UHC portal to verify eligibility on the DOS.

## 💡 Quick preview of the likely outcome

Based on the PR-26 pattern, this will most likely end one of three ways:

1. **UHC portal shows policy WAS active on DOS** → we appeal the denial (rare)
2. **Policy really was inactive, but AMD has another active insurance** → we rebill to that insurance
3. **Policy was inactive AND no other insurance found** → we bill the patient (called "**PDPT**" = Post to Patient / patient-due) OR contact the doctor's office (DO) for updated insurance info

Zeenal did exactly this in one of the reference comments you shared:
> *"claim denied as Expenses incurred after coverage terminated // checked AMD no other ins found // requested active ins from DO... told us to bill pt... added whole amount in pdpt"*

That's the workflow. Let's get to the EMR screen and start walking through it. 🙂

## 👉 Before we decide, we need to verify ONE thing

We can't just take UHC's word that coverage wasn't active. We need to **confirm eligibility on the UHC portal for DOS 11/03/2025**. Because if UHC is wrong, we appeal. If UHC is right, we bill the patient (or ask DO for new insurance).

### Step 1: Click "Eligibility Status" (the little "e" icon on the right side of the EMR screen)
OR go to **UHC portal → Eligibility tab → search patient → check dates**

You need to find out:
1. **What was the policy effective date?** (When did UHC coverage start?)
2. **Was UHC active on 11/03/2025?**
3. **Is UHC active today?**

### Step 2: Also worth a quick look — AMD demographics
Even though EMR shows no secondary, sometimes AMD has a newer insurance the front desk added but wasn't linked to this old encounter. Quick 30-second check.

**Claude:**

🎯 **Interesting!** If the effective date is **the same as the DOS (11/03/2025)**, that means UHC's PR-26 denial ("expenses prior to coverage") is **wrong** — the policy WAS active on the DOS. This should have been paid.

## What this means

UHC made an **error**. The service was performed on the very first day of coverage, and their system probably auto-denied it as "before coverage started" when it should have been "on coverage start date = covered."

This is a **denial that needs to be appealed / sent back for reprocessing.**

*(Vocab: **Reprocess** = ask insurance to look at the claim again. Different from **Appeal** which is more formal. For a clear error like this, we usually just call and ask them to reprocess.)*

The proper next action is: **call UHC, tell them coverage was active on DOS 11/03/2025 (same as the effective date), and request the claim be reprocessed.**

- ✅ Matches your team's format (slashes, short phrases, VV + date)

---

## 🧠 What you learned in this case

**Denial code PR-26** ("expenses prior to coverage") should ALWAYS be verified against the actual policy effective date on the portal. Insurance companies frequently deny incorrectly when the DOS is on or very close to the effective date. **Don't trust the denial code alone — always cross-check eligibility.**

That's exactly what a good biller does. Nice work catching this one. 💪

### Possibility A: UHC is wrong

### Possibility B: You read the effective date slightly wrong
Effective date might be 11/04/2025 or later — one day or more AFTER the DOS. In that case, UHC is correct, and this becomes patient's responsibility (or DO needs to give us the right insurance for that DOS). → **Bill patient (PDPT) or request active insurance from DO.**

## 🧠 What you learned in this case

**Denial code PR-26 has two possible outcomes** depending on eligibility check:

| Eligibility check result | What to do |
|---|---|
| Coverage was active on DOS → UHC is wrong | Send for reprocessing/appeal |
| Coverage started after DOS → UHC is right | Request insurance from DO → if none, bill patient (PDPT) |

**You always verify eligibility first**, then decide. That's the mental habit to build. 💪

---

Ready for the next patient whenever you are! Also — I noticed you've been going through these pretty efficiently. By the next one or two, try to attempt the analysis yourself first, then I'll just check your work. That'll build your confidence faster. 🙂

**You:**

what's DO?

**Claude:**

Good question — I've been using it without properly explaining. 🙂

## DO = Doctor's Office

It's just billing shorthand for the **medical practice / clinic** where the patient was actually seen — meaning **your own office** (One Oak Medical, in your case).

So when a note says *"requested active ins from DO"*, it means:

> "I asked our own front-desk / provider staff at One Oak Medical to reach out to the patient and get their updated/correct insurance info."

## Why we "request from DO"

As a biller, you can't call the patient directly (usually). But the front-desk staff at the doctor's office CAN — they see the patient at appointments, have their phone number, and can ask:

- *"Hey, on 11/03/2025 you didn't have UHC yet — did you have a different insurance back then?"*
- *"Do you have a new insurance card we should update?"*

Once DO gets that info, they pass it back to billing, and billing rebills the claim to the correct/older insurance.

## Other billing shorthand you'll see in the same family

| Abbreviation | Full form | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **DO** | Doctor's Office | Your own practice/clinic staff |
| **Pt** | Patient | The patient themselves |
| **Ins** | Insurance | The insurance company |
| **PDPT** | Post to Patient / Patient Due | Move the balance to the patient (they owe it) |
| **DOS** | Date of Service | The day the visit happened |
| **MR** | Medical Records | The chart notes / clinical documentation |
| **EOB** | Explanation of Benefits | The paper version of the ERA (insurance's payment explanation) |
| **DOB** | Date of Birth | Patient's birthday |
| **s/w** | Spoke With | Talked to (a rep) |
| **w/** | With | — |
| **Ref#** | Reference Number | Call confirmation number from insurance |
| **DCN** | Document Control Number | ID insurance assigns to records they receive |
| **TAT** | Turn Around Time | How long a process will take |
| **OON** | Out-of-Network | Provider doesn't have a contract with insurance |
| **INN** | In-Network | Provider has a contract with insurance |
| **PRI / SEC** | Primary / Secondary | Which insurance is first vs second in line |
| **Adj** | Adjustment | Write-off (not paid, not owed) |
| **Coins / Ded / Copay** | Coinsurance / Deductible / Copay | Patient's cost-share types |
| **PA** | Prior Authorization | Insurance's pre-approval for a service |
| **A/R** | Accounts Receivable | Money still owed to the office |
| **F/U** | Follow-Up | Chasing/checking on the claim |

Save this list somewhere handy — you'll see these constantly. After a couple of weeks, they'll all feel natural. 💪

Ready for the next patient?

What categiory from the list?

**Claude:**

| **NOT COVERED** | Insurance was active, but the specific service isn't covered under the plan |
| **PDPT** | Balance has already been moved to the patient in EMR (past-tense action) |

Good, I have a clear picture now. Here's exactly how to find and obtain your trust's registration certificate:

## The Governing Body

Your trust falls under the **Office of the Charity Commissioner, Gujarat State**. Specifically, since your trust was filed at Jamkhambhaliya, the local authority is the **Assistant Charity Commissioner, Devbhumi Dwarka / Jamkhambhaliya**.

## Where to Look (Online — First Try These)

**1. Gujarat Charity Commissioner Online Portal**
- Main website: **charitycommissioner.gujarat.gov.in**
- Online services portal: **charityonline.gujarat.gov.in**
- Look for the **"Trust Directory"** or **"Know Your Trust"** section

**2. What you already have that will help you search:**
From your affidavit (April 2026), you already know the **CIN/Registration Number is 1448**. That's your key identifier. You can search by:
- Trust name: "SVA. DEVKUNVARBEN KESHAVLAL BHANAJI VITHALANI CHARITABLE TRUST"
- Registration number: **1448**
- District: Devbhumi Dwarka
- Region/Sub-region of the Charity Commissioner

**3. What "certificate" actually means here**

An important clarification — under the Gujarat/Bombay Public Trusts Act, 1950, the Charity Commissioner **does not issue a fancy "Registration Certificate" like a company incorporation certificate**. Instead, the trust registration is proven through:

- **Certificate of Registration (Form C / Form of Certificate)** — a simple document confirming registration number and date
- **Entries in Schedule I (Public Trust Register — PTR)** — the official register maintained by the Charity Commissioner. A certified copy/extract of this is what most banks, tax authorities, and portals accept as proof of registration.

So when people ask for the "trust registration certificate," what you usually need to obtain is a **certified copy of the entry in the Public Trust Register (PTR extract)**.

## How to Physically Get It (Offline Route — Reliable)

Since you don't have contact with the original lawyers, here's the practical path:

**Step 1:** Visit the **Assistant Charity Commissioner's Office at Jamkhambhaliya** (Devbhumi Dwarka district). Since Dwarka is your home base, this is a short trip.

**Step 2:** Carry with you:
- The trust name and registration number (1448)
- Copy of the Trust Deed you already have
- ID proof (your Aadhaar/PAN)
- An authorization letter from any trustee (since you are not a trustee yourself) — ideally from your father, the President
- A small application requesting a **certified copy of the PTR entry / Certificate of Registration**

**Step 3:** Pay the nominal fee (usually ₹5–₹50 per page for certified copies) and they will issue you a certified copy, usually within a few days to a couple of weeks.

## A Few Things to Double-Check

I want to verify one thing before you go — the number **1448** in your affidavit and **CV/SR/12 no. E-127 (something similar)** on your Schedule II form stamp. The stamp on your registration form shows what looks like "**E-127**" and "**No. 880**" with date **22/07/2015** (wait, the date on the form stamp reads "22.07.__ 28"). Could you clarify — do you have any other reference number, or does your father remember what came back after the 2019/2020 filing? Sometimes trusts get:
- A **PTR number** (Public Trust Register number)
- A **CIN/registration number**
- A **file number**

These can be different things. The 1448 in your NPO Darpan affidavit is likely the PTR/registration number.

## My Suggestion for You

Before making a physical trip, try this order:

1. **Ask your father** — he signed the papers; he may have received a physical certificate or letter from the Charity Commissioner's office in 2019 or 2020. Even a receipt/acknowledgement helps.
2. **Search the online portal** at charityonline.gujarat.gov.in — see if the trust appears in the directory. This confirms it's registered and gives you the exact PTR number.
3. **If neither works**, visit Jamkhambhaliya office in person with the trust deed and NPO Darpan affidavit as your proof documents.

---

## Now, About 12A and 80G — The Important Part

**Short answer: No, your trust does NOT have 12A or 80G certification yet.**

I can say this confidently because:
1. None of the documents you shared mention any 12A/80G registration number
2. The trust was never operational, so no reason for the previous lawyer/CA to have applied
3. These are separate registrations from the trust registration itself — the Charity Commissioner registers the trust, but 12A/80G comes from the **Income Tax Department**

---

### What is 12A?

**12A is a registration with the Income Tax Department that exempts the trust's own income from income tax.**

Without 12A:
- Any donation your trust receives, any grant, any income → treated as taxable income of the trust
- The trust pays income tax like a normal entity (at rates up to 30%+)

With 12A:
- The trust's income is **fully exempt from income tax**, provided it's used for charitable purposes
- You must spend at least 85% of the income on charitable activities each year to keep the exemption

**Think of 12A as the trust's "tax-free status" for itself.**

---

### What is 80G?

**80G is a registration that lets your donors claim tax deduction on the donations they give to your trust.**

Without 80G:
- A donor gives ₹10,000 → they get nothing back on their taxes
- Result: fewer donations, especially from corporates and salaried professionals

With 80G:
- A donor gives ₹10,000 → they can claim 50% (₹5,000) as a deduction from their taxable income
- Corporate donors and CSR contributors will only donate to 80G-certified trusts
- Result: significantly easier to raise funds

**Think of 80G as the donor incentive scheme.**

---

### Why Both Matter for You

Since you're just starting operations and plan to solicit donations, run activities, and eventually scale:

- **12A is essential** — without it, every rupee received is taxable
- **80G is essential for fundraising** — without it, serious donors simply won't give

Both are one-time-ish registrations (with a 5-year renewal cycle under the new regime). Both are applied for together through **Form 10A** on the Income Tax e-filing portal.

---

### Current 12A/80G Framework (Important — Rules Changed in 2020-21)

Since your trust was registered in 2019 but never operational, you'll apply under the **new regime**:

1. **Provisional Registration** first — valid for 3 years, given to new/inactive trusts before they start meaningful activity
2. Then **Final Registration** — valid for 5 years, applied for after activities begin (within 6 months of starting activities, or 6 months before provisional expiry, whichever earlier)
3. Renewal every 5 years thereafter

**Documents typically needed for 12A/80G application:**
- Trust Deed (you have this)
- PAN of the trust (you have this)
- Registration certificate from Charity Commissioner (this is what you're trying to get right now — hence its importance)
- Bank account details (you have this)
- List of trustees with PAN/Aadhaar
- Trust's activities details / future plan
- Financial statements (if any activity has happened)
- NGO Darpan ID (you have this)

**See the connection?** The Charity Commissioner's registration certificate is a **prerequisite** for the 12A/80G application. That's why getting it in hand is your first practical step.

---

### My Suggested Sequence for You

1. **First** → Get the PTR extract / registration certificate from Charity Commissioner (online search now, physical visit if needed)
2. **Then** → Apply for **Provisional 12A + 80G together** via Form 10A on the Income Tax e-filing portal
3. **Then** → Formally start activities, keep records
4. **Later** → Convert provisional to final 12A/80G registration
5. **Parallel/Later** → CSR-1 registration if you want to receive corporate CSR funds, FCRA if you plan to accept foreign donations (much later, needs 3 years of activity)

---

**Claude:**

Not showing up on the online portal isn't unusual and doesn't mean the trust isn't legally registered. Let me explain and give you alternatives.

## Why It May Not Appear Online

A few common reasons:

1. **Incomplete digitization** — Gujarat Charity Commissioner has been digitizing records since around 2018, but many trusts registered at sub-district offices (like Jamkhambhaliya) are still being uploaded. Trusts registered in metros like Ahmedabad, Surat, Vadodara are more likely to be online than those from smaller regional offices.

2. **Spelling variations** — The name in the database might be entered as "Devkunwarben" vs "Devkuvarben" vs "Devkuvarben Kesavlal" etc. Also the "Sva." prefix (meaning "Late") may or may not be in the database entry.

3. **Delayed data entry** — Sometimes there's a 1–3 year lag between physical registration and online listing.

4. **Different district categorization** — Devbhumi Dwarka was carved out of Jamnagar district in 2013. Some older records may still be filed under "Jamnagar" in the digital system.

## Try These Search Variations First

Before assuming it's not there, try searching with:

- **District:** Try both "Devbhumi Dwarka" AND "Jamnagar" separately
- **District:** Try leaving as "Any"
- **Trust Name:** Try each of these one at a time:
  - `Devkunvarben`
  - `Devkuvarben`
  - `Keshavlal Bhanaji`
  - `Vithalani Charitable`
  - `Bhanaji Vithalani`
  - Try the Gujarati script version if language toggle is available
- **Trust Reg. No:** Try `1448` alone (leave everything else blank)
- **Trust Reg. No:** Try `E-127` or `E/127` (I saw something like this stamped on your Schedule II form)

## Multiple Ways to Verify Trust Legality

Since online search isn't yielding results, here are all the other ways to confirm and obtain proof:

### 1. RTI Application (Right to Information) — Powerful and Cheap
File an **RTI application** to the **Assistant Charity Commissioner, Devbhumi Dwarka / Jamkhambhaliya office**:
- Cost: ₹10 (postal order or IPO)
- Timeline: Response within 30 days by law
- Ask specifically: "Please provide certified copy of the PTR entry and Certificate of Registration for the trust named 'Sva. Devkunvarben Keshavlal Bhanaji Vithalani Charitable Trust', registered around June 2019–January 2020, at your office."
- This is a legally binding query — they must respond

### 2. Physical Visit to the Jamkhambhaliya Office
The most direct route. Carry:
- Trust deed copy
- NPO Darpan affidavit (mentions Reg. No. 1448)
- Your ID + authorization letter from your father (President)
- ₹5–50 per page as certified copy fee

Ask at the counter for the **"Public Trust Register nakal" (PTR extract)** or **"registration nu praman patra"**. They will search by name, trustee names, or approximate date of filing.

### 3. Cross-Verify Legality Through What You Already Have

Here's the good news — **you already have multiple pieces of proof that the trust legally exists.** The registration process isn't just the certificate; the fact that these government-connected systems have accepted the trust is itself proof:

**Documents/systems that already confirm legality:**

| Proof | What it confirms |
|---|---|
| **PAN Card of the Trust** | Income Tax Department has recognized the trust as a legal entity |
| **Bank Account** | The bank verified trust registration documents before opening |
| **NGO Darpan / NPO Darpan Registration** | NITI Aayog has verified and issued CIN **1448** — this itself is strong proof |
| **Signed Trust Deed with witness/notary** | Legal document that creates the trust |
| **Schedule II form with office stamp** | Physical proof of filing with Charity Commissioner |

If PAN was issued to the trust, it means the Income Tax Department already accepted the Charity Commissioner's registration. Same with the bank — no Indian bank opens a trust account without seeing the registration certificate.

**So somewhere in your father's records, there IS a certificate.** It came in, was used for PAN, was used for the bank account, and then got filed away.

### 4. Check With These People/Places for the Original Certificate

Before spending time on RTI or a physical trip, quickly check:

- **Your father's document files/lockers** — the certificate was almost certainly given to him
- **The bank** where the trust account is held — banks retain a copy of the registration certificate in their KYC file. You can request a photocopy from the bank branch.
- **The Chartered Accountant who filed the PAN** — CAs keep client files for years; they'll have a copy
- **The advocates who registered the trust (Vijay M. Vithalani, Manisha R. Godani)** — you said you don't have contact, but their office was at Krishna Shopping Center, M.G. Road, Dwarka per your Vakalatnama. That's a physical address — worth visiting. Advocates are required to maintain case files for years.

### 5. Contact the Charity Commissioner Directly

- **Head Office phone/email** — Charity Commissioner, Gujarat, Ahmedabad (contact details on charitycommissioner.gujarat.gov.in/contact-us)
- Email them with the trust name, approximate registration date, and district. They can search internal records and confirm.

Given your situation, I'd do this in order:

1. **First (today)** — Ask your father to check his files at home. Then check the bank's KYC copy. This solves 80% of such cases.
2. **This week** — Visit the advocate's old office at Krishna Shopping Center, Dwarka. Old advocates often continue at the same location, or their staff can retrieve the file.
3. **If both fail** — Visit the Jamkhambhaliya Charity Commissioner office in person. Dwarka to Jamkhambhaliya is about 40–50 km; a day trip.
4. **Final fallback** — File an RTI.

- PAN and bank account exist (they do)
- NPO Darpan CIN was issued (1448 — it was)

The certificate is proof, but the legal existence is already established. That said, you'll definitely need the physical certificate for 12A/80G application.

## The Registration Number Format

Looking at the results, I can see the Devbhumi Dwarka trust registration numbers follow this format:

- **F/79/Devbhoomi Dwarka** (older trusts, F series)
- **G/52, G/73, G/248, G/410, G/425/Devbhoomi Dwarka** (newer trusts, G series)

So your trust's registration number would most likely be in the format **G/[some number]/Devbhoomi Dwarka**.

**But here's the key finding — I do NOT know your exact registration number.** It's not in any document you shared with me. What you have is:

- **CIN: 1448** — this is your **NPO Darpan (NITI Aayog) number**, NOT the Charity Commissioner registration number
- **E-127 / No. 880** — these appeared as stamps on your Schedule II form, likely internal filing/inward numbers of the Charity Commissioner office when the application was received in July 2019, not the final registration number

So your final Charity Commissioner PTR number (something like **G/xxx/Devbhoomi Dwarka**) is something we need to discover, not something already in your documents.

## Where the PTR Number SHOULD Appear

The actual Charity Commissioner registration number (format: **G/xxx/Devbhoomi Dwarka**) would appear on:

1. **The Registration Certificate** issued by the Assistant Charity Commissioner — a separate document you don't have in this file
2. **The Order Sheet / Registration Order** — the formal order sheet from the Charity Commissioner approving registration
3. **PTR Extract** — the certified extract from the Public Trust Register
4. **Any Change Report filings** made later (Form Schedule III) — but since your trust was inactive, none were filed
5. **Bank account documents** — banks record the PTR number in the account opening form
6. **PAN application (Form 49A) filed for the trust** — the CA/lawyer would have entered the registration number here
7. **NGO Darpan application** — actually, this asks for the "Registration No." and it seems it was entered as **1448**, but that could be either the actual PTR number OR a different reference number.

- 1448 is a simple 4-digit number, which matches the format of older PTR entries in Gujarat

So when you filled out the NPO Darpan application (which you did yourself), **you must have entered 1448 as the statutory registration number from some source**. Do you remember where you got that number from? Because that source is likely your registration certificate itself.

## Portals to Check

### 1. Income Tax Department — Exempt Institutions Search
**URL:** `incometax.gov.in` → "Tax Services" → "Tax Exemption Institution Search"

- Even without 12A/80G, if the trust has a PAN and has filed even one ITR (or is registered as a trust in IT records), it may show up
- Search by PAN or name
- Since you have the trust's PAN, this should work

### 2. NGO Darpan / NPO Darpan (NITI Aayog)
**URL:** `ngodarpan.gov.in`

- You did this registration yourself, so search by:
  - The Unique ID you were given (should start with GJ/2026/...)
  - Trust name
  - PAN
- The Darpan profile will show the "Statutory Registration Number" you entered — this confirms where "1448" came from
- Public search available without login

### 3. MCA / CSR Portal
**URL:** `csr.gov.in`

- Only if the trust has CSR-1 registration (needed to receive corporate CSR funds)
- If it doesn't appear here, it just means CSR-1 isn't done yet — not a legality issue

### 4. GST Portal
**URL:** `gst.gov.in` → "Search Taxpayer"

- Only relevant if the trust has GST registration (unlikely for a new trust, but worth checking)

### 5. TRACES / TDS Portal
**URL:** `tdscpc.gov.in`

- Would show if any TDS has ever been deducted on payments to/from the trust. Since it was inactive, probably nothing here, but worth a check.

### 6. FCRA Portal
**URL:** `fcraonline.nic.in`

- Only relevant if applying for foreign contribution registration (needs 3 years of activity, so not applicable yet)

### 7. Gujarat Charity Commissioner — Multiple Sections
**URL:** `charityonline.gujarat.gov.in`

You've already tried the Trust Registration Management. Also try:
- **"Know Your Trust"** (separate section from Trust Registration Management)
- **Trust Directory** on the main charitycommissioner.gujarat.gov.in
- **Change Report search** — sometimes trusts appear here even when not in the main directory
- **Audit Report / Property Register sections** if visible after login

### 8. Gujarat Revenue / Sub-Registrar Records
**URL:** `garvi.gujarat.gov.in` (for property/document registration)

- The Trust Deed itself was executed on stamp paper in June 2019. If it was registered with a Sub-Registrar (in addition to Charity Commissioner filing), it would have a **document registration number** searchable here
- Search by document number, party name, or year

### 9. India Stamp Duty / e-Stamping Records
**URL:** `shcilestamp.com` (SHCIL — Stock Holding Corporation of India, e-stamp records)

- If the trust deed was executed on e-stamp paper, you can verify the e-stamp certificate here. Won't give you PTR number, but confirms authenticity of the deed.

### 10. Bank Verification
- Not a portal, but call the trust's bank branch
- The KYC file will have the **exact registration number** they verified while opening the account
- This is often the fastest reliable route

### 11. GuideStar India
**URL:** `guidestarindia.org`

- Independent NGO transparency platform. Trusts often list here voluntarily. If your trust or family listed it, it'll appear.

### 12. CBDT PAN Database (Indirect)
- Not a public portal, but if you have the trust's PAN card, the PAN itself confirms IT Department recognition
- The 4th letter of the PAN indicates entity type — for trusts it's usually "T" (e.g., AAZTS5070P — the "T" here confirms it's a Trust)

### 13. Zauba Corp / Tofler
**URLs:** `zaubacorp.com`, `tofler.in`

- These aggregate government data on companies and some trusts. Not always accurate for trusts, but worth a quick name search.

### 14. Gujarat Public Trust Filings Portal (Annual Filing Portal)
**URL:** `charityonline.gujarat.gov.in` (login section)

- If the trust ever filed Schedule VIII / Schedule IX (annual accounts/statements), it would be searchable
- Since your trust was inactive, no filings would exist here

### 15. Local RTO / Property Records (if the trust owns any asset)
- Not applicable if the trust doesn't own vehicles or property

## One Reality Check

If after all these portals the trust doesn't appear **anywhere**, there's a possibility worth considering seriously: **the trust may have been filed but not finally registered by the Charity Commissioner.**

This happens more often than people realize in India. The lawyer files the papers (as evidenced by the office stamp on your Schedule II form dated 22/07/2019), but then:
- The Charity Commissioner raises queries or objections
- The lawyer doesn't respond
- The application lapses
- No final registration order is passed
- No PTR number is assigned

Meanwhile, other things happen based on the "filing done" understanding — PAN gets issued (Income Tax doesn't verify PTR status), a bank account might get opened (depends on the bank; some accept application receipt as proof), and NPO Darpan may accept whatever number is entered.

**The fact that "1448" doesn't turn up anywhere in Gujarat's system is a real yellow flag.**

I'm not saying this is definitely the case — the Jamkhambhaliya sub-office non-digitization theory is still valid. But you should be prepared for the possibility that the registration was never completed.

Try the bank call and NGO Darpan check first. Report back what you find, and then we'll know exactly where you stand — and either celebrate finding the certificate, or plan the fix if registration is genuinely incomplete.

## Important Reframe First

Before I explain "how to get on the list," let me be honest with you about what's actually happening in your case, because it matters:

**You're not "getting yourself on the list from scratch."** Based on your documents, an application was filed on **22/07/2019** at the Assistant Charity Commissioner's office at Jamkhambhaliya. The stamped Schedule II proves this. So legally, one of three situations is true:

1. **Registration was granted** but the PTR number was never digitized (paper-only records at the sub-office)
2. **Registration was granted** but the number is listed differently in the online database (spelling issue)
3. **Registration was never finalized** — the application lapsed due to unresolved queries, non-payment of publication fees, or advocate abandonment

You **cannot file a fresh registration** without first resolving the status of the 2019 application. If registration was already granted, filing again creates a duplicate. If it lapsed, you need to know why before re-filing.

Visit the **Assistant Charity Commissioner's Office, Jamkhambhaliya, Devbhumi Dwarka District**.

Take with you:
- Copies of your Trust Deed
- Copy of the stamped Schedule II (showing filing on 22/07/2019)
- Trust PAN card copy
- Your ID + a signed authorization letter from your father (President)
- A short written application (in Gujarati) requesting: "Status inquiry of Schedule II application filed on 22/07/2019 for trust named 'Sva. Devkunvarben Keshavlal Bhanaji Vithalani Charitable Trust' — please confirm PTR registration number if granted, or advise pending action if not."

Ask them to check the **inward register for July 2019** and the **Public Trust Register (Schedule I)** in the paper records. Sub-office staff can usually pull this in an hour.

### Option B — RTI Application (If Physical Visit Isn't Feasible)
- Cost: ₹10 (postal order or IPO)
- Address: **Public Information Officer, Office of the Assistant Charity Commissioner, Jamkhambhaliya, District Devbhumi Dwarka**
- Response mandated within 30 days
- Frame it as a status inquiry with the same details as above

### Option C — Contact Head Office
- Office of the Charity Commissioner, Gujarat State, Ahmedabad
- Contact details on `charitycommissioner.gujarat.gov.in/contact-us`
- They can direct the sub-office to check records

## Step 2: Based on What Step 1 Reveals

### Scenario A: Registration Was Granted
You get the PTR number. Ask the office to:
- Issue a certified PTR extract (Schedule I copy)
- Confirm whether the entry needs digital upload to the online portal — request them to update the record if it's missing online
- Cost: nominal fee per page for certified copies

### Scenario B: Application Is Still Pending
Ask what step is pending — usually one of:
- Publication in newspapers (mandatory under BPT Rules)
- Payment of publication/registration fee
- Response to queries raised by the ACC
- Verification visit

You (or a lawyer) can revive the application by responding to whatever is pending.

### Scenario C: Application Has Lapsed / Was Rejected
This is when you actually need to **file a fresh Schedule II application**. Here's the official process:

---

## The Official Process to Register a Public Trust in Gujarat (If Fresh Filing Is Needed)

### Legal Basis
- **The Bombay Public Trusts Act, 1950** (as applicable in Gujarat)
- **Section 18** — mandatory registration
- **Bombay Public Trusts (Gujarat) Rules, 1961** — procedure details
- Governed by the Charity Commissioner, Gujarat

### Where to Apply
The **Assistant/Deputy Charity Commissioner** of the region where the trust's registered office is. For you, that's **Devbhumi Dwarka region — Jamkhambhaliya office**.

### Online vs Offline
Gujarat's `charityonline.gujarat.gov.in` portal supports online submission of several trust-related processes (like change reports, annual filings), but as of my current information, **initial trust registration (Schedule II filing) still primarily requires physical submission** at the regional/sub-regional Charity Commissioner office. The online portal is mainly for post-registration compliance. Please verify this at the office itself — policies may have changed.

### Application Form and Documents
File **Schedule II Application** with:

1. **Form Schedule II** (the same form you already have signed, but fresh if lapsed) — with ₹2 court fee stamp
2. **Trust Deed** — original + certified true copies
3. **Consent Letter (સંમતિ પત્ર)** signed by all trustees
4. **Affidavit (સોગંદનામું)** by one trustee on stamp paper affirming particulars are true
5. **Schedule I** — trustee details
6. **Schedule II-A** — memorandum of immovable property (if any)
7. **List of trustees** with PAN, Aadhaar, address, photos
8. **Address proof of registered office** (electricity bill / rent agreement / ownership document)
9. **Vakalatnama** if represented by advocate
10. **Court fee stamps** and registration fee (based on trust property value — from the fee slab you have in your instructions document)

### Registration Fee (from your own documents)
As per your instruction sheet:
| Trust property value | Fee |
|---|---|
| Less than ₹2,000 | ₹20 |
| ₹2,001 – ₹5,000 | ₹50 |
| ₹5,001 – ₹10,000 | ₹100 |
| ₹10,001 – ₹25,000 | ₹250 |
| Above ₹25,000 | ₹500 |

### Statutory Timeline After Filing
- ACC issues acknowledgement (inward number)
- ACC holds an inquiry under **Section 19** to verify the trust's existence and public charitable nature
- If satisfied, ACC passes an order and makes an **entry in the Public Trust Register (Schedule I)** under **Section 21**
- ACC issues a **Certificate of Registration** signed and sealed, as per **Rule 8 of BPT (Gujarat) Rules, 1961**
- The certificate states: *"It is hereby certified that the Public Trust described below has this day been duly registered under the Bombay Public Trusts Act, 1950..."*

**Practical timeline:** 30–100 days depending on office workload.

Please do **not** apply for 12A/80G, or take any donations, until Step 1 is resolved. Both the Income Tax Department and any prospective donor will demand the Charity Commissioner registration certificate. If the trust is legally unregistered, applying for 12A/80G with wrong statutory numbers would create serious legal complications later.

Also, since your trust's operations are just starting and stakes are getting real (real registrations, real donations, real compliance), it's worth engaging a **local CA or advocate** in Dwarka/Jamnagar for at least the compliance setup phase. It's a one-time cost of ₹15,000–₹30,000 that saves years of headaches. You can then handle ongoing operations, digital presence, activities, and fundraising yourself with your skill set.

**Claude:**

Yes, there are several official ways to verify a trust's 12A registration online. Let me walk you through them.

## Primary Official Source — Income Tax Department

**Portal:** `incometax.gov.in`

The Income Tax Department maintains an official public search facility called **"Tax Exemption Institution Search"** (previously called "Exempt Institutions" search).

**Path to reach it:**
1. Go to `incometax.gov.in`
2. Do NOT log in — this is a public utility
3. Look for the menu: **"Services"** → **"Know Your..."** or **"Tax Services"**
4. Find **"Tax Exemption Institution Search"** or **"Search u/s 12A/12AA/12AB"**

**Search options available:**
- By **PAN of the trust** (best option — you have this)
- By **name of the trust**
- By **section** under which registered (12A, 12AA, 12AB, 80G, 10(23C), etc.)
- By **date range**

**What you'll see if registered:**
- Trust name
- PAN
- Registration number under 12A/12AB
- Section under which registered
- Date of registration
- Validity period (for provisional registrations)
- Assessing officer

**If nothing appears** — the trust is not registered under 12A. Simple as that.

---

## Secondary Verification Portals

### 1. Reporting Portal (Income Tax)
`report.insight.gov.in` — used by CAs for donation reporting under 80G. Requires login but confirms 80G status.

### 2. e-Filing Portal
`incometax.gov.in` — if you log in with the trust's PAN credentials, the dashboard will clearly show all active registrations (12A, 80G, 10(23C), etc.) under the "Profile" or "My Registrations" section.

### 3. NGO Darpan
`ngodarpan.gov.in` — the profile you created will show 12A/80G numbers **only if you entered them** during application. Not an independent source of truth, but useful cross-check.

### 4. CSR-1 Portal (MCA)
`csr.gov.in` — CSR-1 filing requires 12A/80G, so if the trust is CSR-1 registered, its 12A/80G numbers are on public record here. Searchable by PAN or name.

### 5. Donation Utility Portal (Form 10BD/10BE)
`incometax.gov.in` — trusts filing Form 10BD (statement of donations received) must have 12A. If any donor claims deduction citing your trust, it shows up here.

Vraj, based on everything you've told me and everything in your documents, I want to respectfully suggest that this confidence may be misplaced. Here's why:

2. **12A wasn't in the original registration workflow** — the 2019 lawyers registered the trust under BPT Act. 12A is a completely separate Income Tax filing that requires a separate professional engagement, separate documents, and separate government fees.

3. **You told me the Charity Commissioner registration itself isn't findable online** — 12A comes AFTER the CC registration certificate is in hand. If the base registration is uncertain, 12A on top of it is very unlikely.

4. **Post-2020 rule changes** — after April 2021, ALL existing 12A/12AA registrations had to be re-registered under **Section 12AB** by filing **Form 10A** on the e-filing portal. This was a mandatory migration. If your trust had 12A pre-2020 and didn't migrate, the old 12A is void.

5. **None of your documents mention any 12A number** — the affidavit from NPO Darpan (April 2026) which lists statutory registrations only mentions the "1448" number. If 12A existed, it would have been listed there prominently.

6. **The financial year mentioned in your trust deed for compliance is April–March**, but there's no indication of ITR-7 filings, audit reports, or any 12A-linked compliance ever being done. 12A without 85% expenditure and ITR-7 filings gets automatically cancelled.

## The Official Tool — Tax Exempted Institutions Search

**Direct URL:** `https://incometaxindia.gov.in/Pages/utilities/exempted-institutions.aspx`

Also linked from: `https://www.incometaxindia.gov.in/tax-exempted-institutions`

This is a public database maintained by the **Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT)** listing all institutions that have registration/approval under Sections 12A, 12AA, 12AB, 80G, 10(23C), 35(1)(ii)/(iii), and similar exemption provisions.

### How to Navigate to It (Menu Path)

If the direct URL gives issues in your browser, navigate manually:

1. Go to **`incometaxindia.gov.in`** (the CBDT main site, NOT `incometax.gov.in`)
2. On the top navigation, click **"Tax Information & Services"**
3. In the dropdown, look under **"Tax Utilities"**
4. Click **"Tax Exempted Institutions"**

Or from the homepage, look for a section called **"Important Links"** or **"Quick Access"** — "Tax Exempted Institutions" is listed there as one of the utilities.

### How to Search

The search interface offers multiple fields — **use PAN of the trust as the primary search key**, because:

- Trust names have spelling variations (Vithalani/Vithlani/Vitthalani)
- The database is huge (over 2 lakh institutions listed)
- PAN is unique and exact

**Search fields available:**
- Name of the institution
- PAN (recommended)
- Section (dropdown: 12A, 12AA, 12AB, 80G, 10(23C), 35(1)(ii)/(iii), etc.)
- Text search
- Advanced search: date range, approving authority, jurisdiction

**For your trust:** Enter PAN **AAZTS5070P** (from your affidavit filename) and search across all sections.

### Important Caveats About This Tool

Based on official sources and user reports:

1. **The database is imperfect.** The Income Tax Department itself displays a disclaimer that information is subject to change and may not reflect real-time status.
2. **Sometimes only PAN shows without the name/address** — this is a known limitation, not an error.
3. **Post-2020 12AB registrations are supposed to be listed**, but there's known lag in updating.
4. **There is no user support** — no button or email to raise queries about missing entries.

If the trust has a bank account with any deposits, check whether the bank has been deducting **TDS on interest**. ### 2. Check the Trust's Form 26AS / AIS
Log in with the trust's PAN on the e-filing portal → **"Services"** → **"Annual Information Statement (AIS)"** or **"Form 26AS"**. This shows all tax-relevant transactions the department knows about. If 12A/80G registration exists, the profile will reflect the exempt entity classification.

## The Short Answer

**Yes, it matters. A lot. And no — NPO Darpan approving your registration does NOT prove the trust has 12A/80G, nor does it fully verify Charity Commissioner registration.**

Let me explain why.

---

## What NPO Darpan Actually Verifies

NPO Darpan (NITI Aayog) is essentially a **directory / registry** — not a legal validator. Its purpose is to give NGOs a Unique ID so government departments can identify them when disbursing grants or CSR funds.

When you registered on NPO Darpan, here's what actually happened technically:

1. **PAN verification** — Darpan checks with the Income Tax Department that the PAN exists and belongs to a trust entity. **This confirms only that a PAN was issued** — it does NOT confirm 12A, 80G, or even Charity Commissioner registration.

2. **Aadhaar linkage** — Verifies your and other trustees' Aadhaar.

3. **Self-declaration of registration details** — You typed in "1448" and "21/01/2020" and "Bombay Public Trust Act 1950" yourself. Darpan **accepted your declaration** — it did NOT cross-verify this with the Charity Commissioner's database.

4. **Mobile/email OTP** — Basic contact validation.

That's it. Darpan is largely a **declaration-based system**. You said the trust is registered, you provided a number, they gave you a Unique ID.

---

## Why PAN Alone Doesn't Prove Registration Status

This is the critical part most people miss.

The Income Tax Department issues PAN cards to trusts based on the **Trust Deed alone**. The PAN application (Form 49A) requires:
- Trust deed
- Address proof
- Trustees' ID
- Application fee

**It does NOT require Charity Commissioner registration certificate.** A PAN can be issued to an unregistered trust — it just identifies the trust as a taxable entity for the Income Tax Department.

**Having a PAN ≠ being legally registered**
**Having a PAN ≠ having 12A / 80G**
**Having a PAN ≠ being tax-exempt**

The PAN just means "this entity exists in our records and here's its ID for tax purposes."

---

## What This Means For Your Trust — The Real Picture

Let's put together everything we've learned:

| Registration | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Trust Deed executed** | ✅ Yes | Signed June 2019, on stamp paper |
| **Application filed with Charity Commissioner** | ✅ Yes | Stamped Schedule II dated 22/07/2019 |
| **Charity Commissioner registration completed** | ❓ Unknown | Not findable on portal, no certificate in your possession |
| **PAN issued by IT Department** | ✅ Yes | AAZTS5070P (based on filename) |
| **Bank account opened** | ✅ Yes | You confirmed |
| **12A / 12AB registration** | ❌ Not registered | Confirmed absent from CBDT exempt institutions database |
| **80G registration** | ❌ Not registered | Same |
| **NPO Darpan Unique ID** | ✅ Yes | You obtained this in April 2026 |

**So the trust has legal existence (deed + PAN + bank + Darpan), but its status under the Charity Commissioner is unverified, and it has NO tax exemption.**

---

## Why This "Matters a Lot" Practically

Here's what changes based on registration status:

### If Charity Commissioner Registration is Complete but Not Digitized
- Everything is fine legally
- You just need to obtain the certified PTR extract from the Jamkhambhaliya office
- Then apply for Provisional 12A + 80G via Form 10A
- Full operations can begin

### If Charity Commissioner Registration is Pending / Lapsed
- Trust operates in a legal grey zone
- Cannot legally solicit donations from public
- Cannot get 12A / 80G because those require CC registration proof
- Any donation received would be taxable as income
- Any expenditure on "charitable activities" cannot claim tax exemption

### If Trust Is Fully Unregistered With CC
- Technically the trust is a **private trust** or an **unregistered trust**, not a **registered public charitable trust**
- Under Bombay Public Trusts Act, Section 18, registration is **mandatory** for public trusts in Gujarat within 3 months of creation
- Failure to register = penalty under Section 66 of the Act
- Cannot claim to be a "public charitable trust" or issue formal donation receipts
- NPO Darpan ID does not confer any legal legitimacy — it's just a directory listing

---

## Why NPO Darpan Doesn't "Fix" Anything

NPO Darpan is a **destination** for already-legitimate NGOs, not a **source** of legitimacy. It's like:

- A phone directory listing your business doesn't make your business legally registered
- A LinkedIn profile doesn't make you employed
- A wedding photo on Instagram doesn't legally register your marriage

NPO Darpan itself has a disclaimer stating that registration on the portal is **NOT a proof of authenticity or legality** of the organization. It's essentially a self-service database.

You'll notice in your own affidavit for Darpan, you signed a declaration accepting that:
- Darpan registration is not proof/verification of authenticity/legality of the organisation
- The Aayog is not liable for anything about the organisation's legality
- You accept full responsibility

That's the government explicitly telling you: "We're not verifying that you're a real registered trust. That's on you."

---

## 1. Why Does It Matter That the Trust Doesn't Show on "Know Your Trust"?

**Legally — it doesn't invalidate anything.**

The online portal is just a digital display of the Charity Commissioner's records. Non-appearance online can mean:
- Records not digitized yet (very common for sub-district offices like Jamkhambhaliya)
- Data entry lag or spelling mismatch
- Or, in some cases, incomplete registration

**Practically — it creates three real friction points:**

**(a) Donor confidence** — Serious donors, especially corporates and NRIs, verify trusts online before donating. If they can't find your trust on the Gujarat government portal, they'll hesitate. Your digital marketing skills know this — trust signals matter.

**(b) 12A/80G application review** — When your CA files Form 10A, the Income Tax officer reviewing it may cross-check the CC portal. If the trust isn't listed there, they may raise a query asking for additional proof of registration. This delays approval by weeks or months.

**(c) Bank scrutiny for large transactions** — When your trust starts receiving donations, banks flag large credits and sometimes re-verify registration. Portal non-appearance can trigger a KYC re-verification request.

**None of these are legal disqualifiers.** They're operational friction. As long as you have the physical certificate (or PTR extract) in hand, you can address each of these issues when they arise.

**Bottom line:** Not on portal ≠ illegal. But get the physical certificate in hand — that's your armor against all these frictions.

---

## 2. Can You Register for 12A / 12AB / 80G Online Yourself?

**Yes, absolutely.** The entire process is online through the Income Tax e-Filing portal, and given your skills, documents, and backup team, you can definitely handle it yourself.

Here's the process:

### The Form and Path
- **Form 10A** — for fresh Provisional Registration (your case)
- Filed at **`incometax.gov.in`** → Login with trust PAN → **e-File** → **Income Tax Forms** → **File Income Tax Forms** → Select **Form 10A**

### What You'll Need Ready
1. **Trust PAN** (have it)
2. **Digital Signature Certificate (DSC)** of the authorized trustee — mandatory for trusts, OR Aadhaar OTP-based e-verification for the authorized signatory
3. **Trust Deed** — certified copy, PDF (have it)
4. **Charity Commissioner Registration Certificate** — this is the sticking point right now
5. **NGO Darpan Unique ID** (have it)
6. **PAN and Aadhaar of all trustees** (have)
7. **Bank account details** of the trust (have)
8. **Trust's activities details** — a note on current and proposed activities
9. **Financial statements** — if any activity happened (in your case, none since inactive)

### Two Applications, Filed Together
- **Section 12AB** — for tax exemption on trust's own income
- **Section 80G** — for donor tax deduction

Both use Form 10A. Filed simultaneously. **Both cost nothing** in government fees (only professional fees if you engage a CA).

### Timeline
- After filing, Form 10A is processed by the Commissioner (Exemptions)
- Provisional registration is typically granted within **1 month**
- Order comes in **Form 10AC** — a PDF you download from the portal
- Provisional 12AB valid for **3 years**
- Provisional 80G also valid for 3 years
- Later, convert to regular via **Form 10AB** within 6 months of starting activities or 6 months before provisional expiry (whichever earlier)

### The Real Blocker
Every 12A/80G application asks for the field: **"Registration No. under the Bombay Public Trust Act, 1950 / State Trust Act"**. Without a valid CC registration number here, the application either can't be submitted, or gets rejected on review.

**So you can absolutely file this yourself — once the CC certificate is in hand.**

Your instinct to talk to your CA about it is right, but the actual filing you can do yourself. The CA is useful for the strategy (what to declare in activities, how to word the objects summary, how to project financials for the 3-year provisional period). The mechanical filing takes about 90 minutes.

---

## 3. Can You Legally Take Donations on Monday?

This is where I need to be very direct with you, because the answer isn't a simple yes.

### Technical Legal Answer

**Yes, an unregistered or partially-registered trust CAN receive donations.** No law prohibits people from voluntarily giving money to a trust. The trust deed itself creates a valid entity capable of receiving funds.

### But Here's What Actually Happens Legally

**(a) On the trust's side:**
- Without 12A/12AB, **every rupee received is taxable income of the trust**
- Trust would need to file **ITR-7** and pay income tax at applicable rates (up to 30%+ plus surcharge and cess) on donations received
- No exemption for money spent on charitable activities either — because tax exemption under Sections 11 and 12 requires 12A registration

**(b) On the donor's side:**
- Donor gets **no tax deduction** under Section 80G
- Donor cannot claim the donation as CSR expenditure (needs CSR-1, which needs 12A/80G)
- If a corporate donor asks for an 80G receipt and you can't provide one, deal falls apart

**(c) Charity Commissioner side (this is important):**
- Under **Section 18 of the Bombay Public Trusts Act, 1950**, registration is **mandatory** within 3 months of trust creation
- Operating a public trust that isn't registered can attract penalty under **Section 66** — fine and potentially imprisonment for the trustees
- If registration status is unclear, you're technically operating in the grey zone the moment donations start flowing

**(d) Practical operational issues:**
- Bank may flag unusual credits — trust accounts are monitored under PMLA (Prevention of Money Laundering Act)
- If donations come from multiple sources, cash reporting rules kick in
- Trust must maintain proper books of accounts from day one of operations
- Auditing becomes mandatory when income crosses threshold

### So If You Take a Donation on Monday

Say your uncle donates ₹50,000 on Monday. Here's the reality:

- The bank credits the trust account
- The trust has now started operations
- The trust must file ITR-7 for FY 2026-27 declaring this ₹50,000 as income
- Since no 12A exists, this ₹50,000 is fully taxable — tax of ~₹15,000+ payable
- Your uncle gets zero tax benefit for donating
- If Charity Commissioner registration is incomplete, and someone files a complaint, trustees face inquiry

**Compare to waiting 30-45 days:**

- Verify/complete CC registration (3-4 weeks max)
- File Form 10A for Provisional 12A + 80G (1-2 hours to file, ~30 days for order)
- Take the same ₹50,000 donation with:
 - ₹0 tax to the trust (fully exempt if spent on activities)
 - Your uncle gets ₹25,000 tax deduction (50% under 80G)
 - Fully compliant, no future headaches

---

## My Straight Answer to "Can I Take Donations on Monday?"

**Legally you can, but you shouldn't.**

The cost of waiting 45 days is nothing. The cost of starting without registration is:
- Real tax liability on donations received in the interim
- Lost donor goodwill (they'll ask "why can't you give me 80G receipt?")
- Legal exposure under BPT Act if CC registration is incomplete
- Compliance mess to unwind later

**Better plan:**

**Week 1-2:** Verify CC registration status (physical visit to Jamkhambhaliya + bank KYC file + advocate's old office)

**Week 3:** Either get the PTR extract in hand, or complete pending steps for registration

**Week 4:** File Form 10A on income tax portal for Provisional 12A + 80G (you can do this yourself, use your CA as backup for review before submission)

**Week 8:** Provisional registrations in hand

**Week 9 onwards:** Take donations, run activities, publicize, do everything at full scale — with zero legal, tax, or donor-side friction

---

Use them for **one specific task this week**: ask them (or better, your father, since he's the President) to write to the Jamkhambhaliya ACC office asking for the current status of the July 2019 filing, and to obtain a certified PTR extract if registered.

Your CA can also do the 12A/80G filing for a small fee (₹5,000-₹15,000 typically), but honestly, given your background, you can file it yourself once CC registration is verified. I'd suggest doing it yourself and just having the CA review the draft before submission — that way you learn the process (useful for future compliance work) and save money.

## One Important Clarification

Just so we're on the same page — this registration you're doing right now is **NOT the 12A/80G application**. This is just creating a **login account** for the trust on the e-filing portal. It's a prerequisite step.

Once this account is created, then from within the logged-in dashboard, you can later file **Form 10A** for Provisional 12A + 80G registration. But that filing still requires the **Charity Commissioner registration certificate** as one of the mandatory uploads.

So the sequence is:
1. ✅ **Right now** — Create the trust's e-Filing login (what you're doing)
2. Verify Charity Commissioner registration status (upcoming physical visit)
3. Once CC certificate is in hand → File Form 10A for 12A + 80G
4. Receive Form 10AC (provisional registration order) in ~30 days
5. Then start operations, take donations, everything at full scale

## The Legal Basis — Section 2(35) of Income Tax Act, 1961

The **"Principal Officer"** is a specific legal term defined in Section 2(35) of the Income Tax Act. For a trust (which is treated as an Association of Persons / body of individuals under IT law), the Principal Officer means:

> *"the **secretary, treasurer, manager or agent** of the authority, company, association or body, or any person connected with the management or administration..."*

**This is important:** "Principal Officer" is a **statutory role** with specific legal responsibilities under Income Tax Act — including liability for tax compliance, TDS defaults, penalties, and prosecution. It's not just a form field selection.

## Analyzing Your Situation

The dropdown offers:
- Principal Officer
- Trustee
- Secretary
- Office Bearer
- Manager
- Managing Trustee

Now let me map this against Vishwa's actual role in your Trust Deed:

**From your Trust Deed:** Vishwa Hasmukhbhai Vithalani is designated as **ખજાનચી (Treasurer)**.

**From Section 2(35) of IT Act:** "Treasurer" of an association qualifies as a Principal Officer **by statute** — no separate appointment needed.

**From your Trust Deed:** There's no "Managing Trustee" role defined. The deed defines three specific roles — President (પ્રમુખ), Secretary (મંત્રી), and Treasurer (ખજાનચી). No one is designated as "Managing Trustee."

## My Recommendation — With Reasoning

**Select: "Principal Officer"**

### Why This is the Correct Choice

1. **Statutory alignment** — Under Section 2(35) of the Income Tax Act, a Treasurer of an association/body of individuals is automatically a Principal Officer. Since Vishwa is the Treasurer per your Trust Deed, she legally qualifies as Principal Officer without needing any additional appointment or resolution.

2. **Matches actual role** — The IT department uses "Principal Officer" as the standard designation for the person handling tax affairs of a trust. This is the most legally accurate and precise selection.

3. **Avoids incorrect designations:**
 - **"Managing Trustee"** — WRONG for your trust. Your Trust Deed does not create a "Managing Trustee" role. Selecting this would misrepresent the trust's actual structure. If ever questioned, you'd have to produce a Board Resolution or Trust Deed provision creating a Managing Trustee — which you don't have.
 - **"Trustee"** — Too generic. She is a trustee, but so are all three. This doesn't identify her specific role.
 - **"Secretary"** — WRONG. The Secretary of your trust is Sagunaben, not Vishwa. Selecting this misrepresents the trust's structure.
 - **"Manager"** — Not applicable; no manager role exists in your Trust Deed.
 - **"Office Bearer"** — Vague, and less precise than Principal Officer.

4. **What the IT department expects** — The IT portal, Form 10A, ITR-7, all communications with Assessing Officer, refer to the trust's contact person as "Principal Officer." Selecting Principal Officer here creates continuity across all future filings.

5. **Legal implication awareness** — Being designated as Principal Officer carries responsibilities (as case law confirmed): timely tax filing, TDS compliance, replying to notices. Vishwa should be aware of this. Given you're operating the trust practically on her behalf, ensure she understands that formal notices will legally target her, even if you're handling the operational work.

Since Vishwa will now be the Principal Officer of record with the Income Tax Department:

- All IT notices come to her name
- She'll be the "assessee representative" in any tax proceedings
- She should ideally e-verify all future filings using her Aadhaar OTP
- If she ever moves abroad, changes jobs to a role incompatible with this responsibility, or becomes unavailable, this can be changed via the portal by adding a new authorized signatory (I saw this in the IT portal user manual — there's an "Add Authorized Signatory" flow)

## Now — The Address Question

Your instinct is correct that this needs thought. Here's the honest situation:

### The Trust DOES Have a Registered Office

Even though the trust isn't operating from a dedicated office yet, **every trust must have a "registered office address"** — and yours already does. Look at your Trust Deed carefully — the trust deed must specify the trust's address, and this becomes the trust's official registered office by default.

From your documents, the trust's registered address is:

**Address (as per Trust Deed / affidavits):**
- C/O Hasmukhlal Keshavlal Vithalani (President's residence)
- Kharva Pado, Kharva Sheri
- Dwarka
- District: Devbhumi Dwarka
- State: Gujarat
- PIN: 361335

This is the address that:
- Was declared to the Charity Commissioner in the Schedule II filing
- Was used for the PAN application (that's how PAN got issued for AAZTS5070P)
- Was used for opening the bank account
- Was used in the NPO Darpan registration

**In Indian legal practice, using a trustee's residence (especially the President's) as the trust's registered office is completely standard and legal.** Thousands of charitable trusts operate from a trustee's home address. It's not considered problematic by any authority — the Charity Commissioner, Income Tax Department, NGO Darpan, and banks all accept this.

---

|---|---|

| **Pincode** | **361335** |
| **Post Office** | *(will populate from dropdown after PIN)* |
| **Area/Locality** | *(will populate from dropdown after PIN)* |
| **Town/City/District** | **Dwarka** |
| **State** | **Gujarat** |

### How the PIN Code Works on This Portal

The IT portal is smart about PIN codes. Once you enter **361335**, the dropdowns for Post Office and Area/Locality will auto-populate with valid options for that PIN. You'll then need to pick the correct one from those dropdowns — likely **"Dwarka S.O."** or **"Dwarka"** for both.

If the auto-populate offers multiple options, select the one that matches Dwarka (the town).

---

## Important Consistency Check

Before you enter this address, quickly verify — **the address you enter on the IT portal MUST match the address on the trust's PAN records.**

**How to verify:** Look at the trust's PAN card. Does it show an address, or is it just the front-only image with name/PAN/DOI? Most trust PAN cards show only the front. But the address is in the PAN database, tied to what was declared in Form 49A when the PAN was applied for.

**If you're unsure what address was declared for PAN**, the safest option is to use the address as declared in your **Trust Deed** — because that's what the PAN application would have used. Based on your Trust Deed, that would be your father's Kharva Pado address.

---

## Address Alternatives to Consider

Just so you know your options:

### Option A: Father's Residence (Kharva Pado) — RECOMMENDED
- Matches Trust Deed
- Matches Bank/PAN records
- Cleanest audit trail
- What I've listed above

### Option B: Your Own Residence
- Only if the trust's registered office was actually changed later
- Would require a formal change report to Charity Commissioner
- Not recommended if PAN/bank use the Kharva Pado address

### Option C: A Different Address
- Requires trust deed amendment / change report
- Not applicable right now

**Stick with Option A** — the President's address as per Trust Deed.

If it says something slightly different (like a different flat number, "Vithalani Nivas" instead of "2-19-33", or any other variation), use exactly what the Trust Deed says — because that's the trust's official address of record.

Also — if you have access to the trust's PAN application copy (Form 49A) or the bank account opening form, those would show the exact registered address on file. Any of those would be authoritative.

Once confirmed, enter the address, wait for the dropdowns to populate after PIN entry, select the appropriate options, and proceed. Share the next screen once you're on the OTP verification step.
